People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

It doesn’t take over my whole day or anything, but it does slow me down, and I feel less free creatively than I used to.\n\nDoctor: When you re-check your layouts, are you trying to prevent something bad from happening, like a client being upset or your work being rejected?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think that’s part of it… not in a dramatic way, I hope. It’s more like I’m trying to avoid that awful moment where a client notices something I missed, or thinks I was careless. So before I submit work, I’ll re-open the file, compare the layers, re-save it, and sometimes mentally walk through the design choices one more time. It doesn’t take over my whole day, but it does make me slower… and I feel a bit less free creatively, if that makes sense.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like your need to re-check your work is causing problems in your daily life or relationships?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. It doesn’t take over my whole day, but it does make me slower… and I feel a bit less free creatively, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to re-check your work is causing problems in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s ruining things, exactly… I’m still getting my work done. I guess I worry that could hurt my chances of moving toward a creative director role. I mean, I’m not trying to think about it, and I’ll have already checked and it looks okay, but then this thought pops back in like, what if there’s one tiny mistake, or what if I sent the wrong version? I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it is enough that I notice it and wish I could just submit things more easily.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and checking behaviors affecting your relationships with friends or family in any way?\n\nPatient: Not hugely with friends or family, I don’t think… at least not directly. I’m still functioning and getting my work done. It’s more that work is where it shows up — I’ve started missing some internal deadlines because I keep checking, and I’m holding back on bolder design ideas because I’m scared I’ll overlook something and hurt my chances of moving toward a creative director role. So I guess it affects my mood and confidence, which maybe they notice a bit, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe these thoughts about mistakes as popping into your head even when you're trying to think about something else?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. Not constantly, but they do sort of pop back in when I’m trying to move on to something else. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control when these thoughts about mistakes come into your mind?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I can sometimes redirect myself, so it’s not like I’m completely helpless with it. Not constantly, but they do sort of pop back in when I’m trying to move on to something else. Like I’ll be working on another layout, or even just trying to step away, and I get this nagging thought that maybe I missed some tiny mistake or sent the wrong version to a client.\n\nAnd even if I can look at it and know it’s probably fine, my mind kind of circles back to it anyway. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control when these thoughts about mistakes come into your mind?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I can sometimes redirect myself, so it’s not like I’m completely helpless with it. But I don’t feel like I choose when it comes up.\n\nIt’s more like I’ll be trying to move on, and then the thought just comes back — that maybe I missed some tiny mistake in a layout, or sent the wrong version to a client. Even when I can tell myself it’s probably fine, my mind keeps circling back. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these nagging thoughts about mistakes in your work?\n\nPatient: I’m not exactly sure when I’d say it started… it’s been long enough that it’s what made me come in, I guess. The main thing is these nagging thoughts that I’ve missed some tiny mistake in a layout, or maybe sent the wrong version to a client.\n\nEven when I can look at it and think, “No, this is probably fine,” my mind keeps circling back to it. A little, yes… I don’t want to overstate it, because I am still getting my work done. It’s not like everything has fallen apart.

I mean, I’m not trying to think about it, and I’ll have already checked and it looks okay, but then this thought pops back in like, what if there’s one tiny mistake, or what if I sent the wrong version? It feels a little embarrassing to say, because I know it’s probably fine, but my mind just keeps circling back to it anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything over and over, like checking your layouts multiple times, to try and get rid of these thoughts about mistakes?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I try not to make it into a big thing, but before I submit something now I’ll usually re-open the file, compare the layers, re-save it… sometimes I’ll sort of mentally walk through every design choice one more time, just to feel sure. It doesn’t take over my whole day or anything, but it does slow me down, and I feel less free creatively than I used to.\n\nDoctor: When you re-check your layouts, are you trying to prevent something bad from happening, like a client being upset or your work being rejected?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think that’s part of it… not in a dramatic way, I hope. It doesn’t take over my whole day, but it does make me slower… and I feel a bit less free creatively, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to re-check your work is causing problems in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s ruining things, exactly… I’m still getting my work done. Yes… I think so, at least somewhat. I still enjoy parts of the work, and I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function, because I am still getting things done.

I’m a designer, and I keep getting these nagging thoughts that I’ve missed some tiny mistake in a layout, or that I sent the wrong version to a client. Even when I look and it seems fine, my mind kind of keeps circling back to it. I know that may sound a bit silly, but it’s been hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these thoughts about mistakes in your layouts come into your mind against your will?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I’m not trying to think about it, and I’ll have already checked and it looks okay, but then this thought pops back in like, what if there’s one tiny mistake, or what if I sent the wrong version? It feels a little embarrassing to say, because I know it’s probably fine, but my mind just keeps circling back to it anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything over and over, like checking your layouts multiple times, to try and get rid of these thoughts about mistakes?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I try not to make it into a big thing, but before I submit something now I’ll usually re-open the file, compare the layers, re-save it… sometimes I’ll sort of mentally walk through every design choice one more time, just to feel sure. It doesn’t take over my whole day or anything, but it does slow me down, and I feel less free creatively than I used to.\n\nDoctor: When you re-check your layouts, are you trying to prevent something bad from happening, like a client being upset or your work being rejected?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think that’s part of it… not in a dramatic way, I hope. It’s more like I’m trying to avoid that awful moment where a client notices something I missed, or thinks I was careless. So before I submit work, I’ll re-open the file, compare the layers, re-save it, and sometimes mentally walk through the design choices one more time. It doesn’t take over my whole day, but it does make me slower… and I feel a bit less free creatively, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to re-check your work is causing problems in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s ruining things, exactly… I’m still getting my work done. I’ve missed some internal deadlines because I keep doing “one more” check, and I’ve noticed I’m holding back on bolder design ideas because I’m afraid I’ll miss something and look careless. I guess I worry that could hurt my chances of moving toward a creative director role. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it is enough that I notice it and wish I could just submit things more easily.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and checking behaviors affecting your relationships with friends or family in any way?\n\nPatient: Not hugely with friends or family, I don’t think… at least not directly. I’m still functioning and getting my work done. So I guess it affects my mood and confidence, which maybe they notice a bit, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe these thoughts about mistakes as popping into your head even when you're trying to think about something else?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I wouldn’t say it’s ruining things, exactly… I’m still getting my work done, and I don’t think it’s really affecting my personal relationships in a major way.

But at work, yes, it is getting in the way some. I’ve started missing internal deadlines because I keep checking or second-guessing before I send things off. And I’ve noticed I’m holding back on some of the bolder design ideas I used to feel more comfortable trying, because I’m worried I’ll overlook something and it’ll make me look careless.

I guess the distress part is mostly that I’m worried it could hurt my chances of moving toward a creative director role. I don’t want to seem like I’m not ready for that. Does that count as significant?

I mean, I’m not trying to think about it, and I’ll have already checked and it looks okay, but then this thought pops back in like, what if there’s one tiny mistake, or what if I sent the wrong version? It feels a little embarrassing to say, because I know it’s probably fine, but my mind just keeps circling back to it anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything over and over, like checking your layouts multiple times, to try and get rid of these thoughts about mistakes?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I try not to make it into a big thing, but before I submit something now I’ll usually re-open the file, compare the layers, re-save it… sometimes I’ll sort of mentally walk through every design choice one more time, just to feel sure. It doesn’t take over my whole day or anything, but it does slow me down, and I feel less free creatively than I used to.\n\nDoctor: When you re-check your layouts, are you trying to prevent something bad from happening, like a client being upset or your work being rejected?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think that’s part of it… not in a dramatic way, I hope. It doesn’t take over my whole day, but it does make me slower… and I feel a bit less free creatively, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to re-check your work is causing problems in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s ruining things, exactly… I’m still getting my work done. It doesn’t take over my whole day, but it does make me slower… and I feel a bit less free creatively, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to re-check your work is causing problems in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s ruining things, exactly… I’m still getting my work done. I’m a designer, and I keep getting these nagging thoughts that I’ve missed some tiny mistake in a layout, or that I sent the wrong version to a client. Even when I look and it seems fine, my mind kind of keeps circling back to it. I know that may sound a bit silly, but it’s been hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these thoughts about mistakes in your layouts come into your mind against your will?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I’m not trying to think about it, and I’ll have already checked and it looks okay, but then this thought pops back in like, what if there’s one tiny mistake, or what if I sent the wrong version? It feels a little embarrassing to say, because I know it’s probably fine, but my mind just keeps circling back to it anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything over and over, like checking your layouts multiple times, to try and get rid of these thoughts about mistakes?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. It doesn’t take over my whole day or anything, but it does slow me down, and I feel less free creatively than I used to.\n\nDoctor: When you re-check your layouts, are you trying to prevent something bad from happening, like a client being upset or your work being rejected?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think that’s part of it… not in a dramatic way, I hope. It’s more like I’m trying to avoid that awful moment where a client notices something I missed, or thinks I was careless. So before I submit work, I’ll re-open the file, compare the layers, re-save it, and sometimes mentally walk through the design choices one more time. It doesn’t take over my whole day, but it does make me slower… and I feel a bit less free creatively, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to re-check your work is causing problems in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s ruining things, exactly… I’m still getting my work done. But, yes, it is starting to affect me a bit. I’ve missed some internal deadlines because I keep doing “one more” check, and I’ve noticed I’m holding back on bolder design ideas because I’m afraid I’ll miss something and look careless. I guess I worry that could hurt my chances of moving toward a creative director role. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How much distress or anxiety do these thoughts and re-checking behaviors cause you?\n\nPatient: It’s… not extreme, I don’t think. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think it does, at least for a little while. Like before I submit something, I’ll re-open the file, compare the layers, re-save it, and sometimes kind of mentally walk through every design choice again.

It’s not like it takes over my whole day or anything, but it does make me slower. And I guess it makes me feel a little safer in the moment, like I’m less likely to have missed something. But then it also makes me feel less free creatively, if that makes sense.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi… thank you. I guess it’s probably not a huge thing, but it’s been bothering me enough that I thought I should ask someone. I’m a designer, and I keep getting these nagging thoughts that I’ve missed some tiny mistake in a layout, or that I sent the wrong version to a client. Even when I look and it seems fine, my mind kind of keeps circling back to it. I know that may sound a bit silly, but it’s been hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these thoughts about mistakes in your layouts come into your mind against your will?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I’m not trying to think about it, and I’ll have already checked and it looks okay, but then this thought pops back in like, what if there’s one tiny mistake, or what if I sent the wrong version? It feels a little embarrassing to say, because I know it’s probably fine, but my mind just keeps circling back to it anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything over and over, like checking your layouts multiple times, to try and get rid of these thoughts about mistakes?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I try not to make it into a big thing, but before I submit something now I’ll usually re-open the file, compare the layers, re-save it… sometimes I’ll sort of mentally walk through every design choice one more time, just to feel sure. It doesn’t take over my whole day or anything, but it does slow me down, and I feel less free creatively than I used to.\n\nDoctor: When you re-check your layouts, are you trying to prevent something bad from happening, like a client being upset or your work being rejected?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think that’s part of it… not in a dramatic way, I hope. It’s more like I’m trying to avoid that awful moment where a client notices something I missed, or thinks I was careless. So before I submit work, I’ll re-open the file, compare the layers, re-save it, and sometimes mentally walk through the design choices one more time. It doesn’t take over my whole day, but it does make me slower… and I feel a bit less free creatively, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to re-check your work is causing problems in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s ruining things, exactly… I’m still getting my work done. But, yes, it is starting to affect me a bit. I’ve missed some internal deadlines because I keep doing “one more” check, and I’ve noticed I’m holding back on bolder design ideas because I’m afraid I’ll miss something and look careless. I guess I worry that could hurt my chances of moving toward a creative director role. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How much distress or anxiety do these thoughts and re-checking behaviors cause you?\n\nPatient: It’s… not extreme, I don’t think. I mean, I can still function and get things done. But it does make me tense in the moment, like I can’t quite relax until I’ve checked it again. I’d call it more frustrating and uncomfortable than unbearable. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it is enough that I notice it and wish I could just submit things more easily.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and checking behaviors affecting your relationships with friends or family in any way?\n\nPatient: Not hugely with friends or family, I don’t think… at least not directly. I’m still functioning and getting my work done. It’s more that work is where it shows up — I’ve started missing some internal deadlines because I keep checking, and I’m holding back on bolder design ideas because I’m scared I’ll overlook something and hurt my chances of moving toward a creative director role. So I guess it affects my mood and confidence, which maybe they notice a bit, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe these thoughts about mistakes as popping into your head even when you're trying to think about something else?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. Not constantly, but they do sort of pop back in when I’m trying to move on to something else. Like I’ll be working on another layout, or even just trying to step away, and I get this nagging thought that maybe I missed some tiny mistake or sent the wrong version to a client.\n\nAnd even if I can look at it and know it’s probably fine, my mind kind of circles back to it anyway. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control when these thoughts about mistakes come into your mind?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I can sometimes redirect myself, so it’s not like I’m completely helpless with it. But I don’t feel like I choose when it comes up.\n\nIt’s more like I’ll be trying to move on, and then the thought just comes back — that maybe I missed some tiny mistake in a layout, or sent the wrong version to a client. Even when I can tell myself it’s probably fine, my mind keeps circling back. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these nagging thoughts about mistakes in your work?\n\nPatient: I’m not exactly sure when I’d say it started… it’s been long enough that it’s what made me come in, I guess. The main thing is these nagging thoughts that I’ve missed some tiny mistake in a layout, or maybe sent the wrong version to a client.\n\nEven when I can look at it and think, “No, this is probably fine,” my mind keeps circling back to it. I don’t know if that answers the “how long” part very well, sorry.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors are interfering with your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t want to overstate it, because I am still getting my work done. It’s not like everything has fallen apart.\n\nBut I have started missing some internal deadlines, mostly because I keep checking or hesitating before I send things on. And I’ve noticed I’m playing it safer with designs than I used to. Like, I’ll hold back on a bolder idea because I’m worried I’ll miss something and it’ll reflect badly on me.\n\nI guess the part that bothers me is I’m trying to move toward a creative director role eventually, and I worry this is making me look less ready for that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and checking behaviors significantly affecting your ability to enjoy your work or feel confident in your professional abilities?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, at least somewhat. I still enjoy parts of the work, and I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function, because I am still getting things done.\n\nBut it has affected my confidence. I’ve started missing internal deadlines here and there because I’m checking things too much or hesitating before sending them. And I notice I’m choosing safer design ideas instead of the bolder ones I might have tried before, because I’m afraid I’ll miss something and it’ll make me look careless.\n\nI guess the bigger worry is that I want to keep moving toward a creative director role, and I’m scared this is making me seem less capable or less ready for that. I mean… I’m still getting things done. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m failing at work or anything.

But yes, I guess it has started to affect things a bit. I’ve missed some internal deadlines because I keep checking and adjusting before I’m willing to let something go. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi… thank you. I guess it’s probably not a huge thing, but it’s been bothering me enough that I thought I should ask someone. I’m a designer, and I keep getting these nagging thoughts that I’ve missed some tiny mistake in a layout, or that I sent the wrong version to a client. Even when I look and it seems fine, my mind kind of keeps circling back to it. I know that may sound a bit silly, but it’s been hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these thoughts about mistakes in your layouts come into your mind against your will?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I’m not trying to think about it, and I’ll have already checked and it looks okay, but then this thought pops back in like, what if there’s one tiny mistake, or what if I sent the wrong version? It feels a little embarrassing to say, because I know it’s probably fine, but my mind just keeps circling back to it anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything over and over, like checking your layouts multiple times, to try and get rid of these thoughts about mistakes?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I try not to make it into a big thing, but before I submit something now I’ll usually re-open the file, compare the layers, re-save it… sometimes I’ll sort of mentally walk through every design choice one more time, just to feel sure. It doesn’t take over my whole day or anything, but it does slow me down, and I feel less free creatively than I used to.\n\nDoctor: When you re-check your layouts, are you trying to prevent something bad from happening, like a client being upset or your work being rejected?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think that’s part of it… not in a dramatic way, I hope. It’s more like I’m trying to avoid that awful moment where a client notices something I missed, or thinks I was careless. So before I submit work, I’ll re-open the file, compare the layers, re-save it, and sometimes mentally walk through the design choices one more time. It doesn’t take over my whole day, but it does make me slower… and I feel a bit less free creatively, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to re-check your work is causing problems in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s ruining things, exactly… I’m still getting my work done. But, yes, it is starting to affect me a bit. I’ve missed some internal deadlines because I keep doing “one more” check, and I’ve noticed I’m holding back on bolder design ideas because I’m afraid I’ll miss something and look careless. I guess I worry that could hurt my chances of moving toward a creative director role. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How much distress or anxiety do these thoughts and re-checking behaviors cause you?\n\nPatient: It’s… not extreme, I don’t think. I mean, I can still function and get things done. But it does make me tense in the moment, like I can’t quite relax until I’ve checked it again. I’d call it more frustrating and uncomfortable than unbearable. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it is enough that I notice it and wish I could just submit things more easily.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and checking behaviors affecting your relationships with friends or family in any way?\n\nPatient: Not hugely with friends or family, I don’t think… at least not directly. I’m still functioning and getting my work done. It’s more that work is where it shows up — I’ve started missing some internal deadlines because I keep checking, and I’m holding back on bolder design ideas because I’m scared I’ll overlook something and hurt my chances of moving toward a creative director role. So I guess it affects my mood and confidence, which maybe they notice a bit, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe these thoughts about mistakes as popping into your head even when you're trying to think about something else?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. Not constantly, but they do sort of pop back in when I’m trying to move on to something else. Like I’ll be working on another layout, or even just trying to step away, and I get this nagging thought that maybe I missed some tiny mistake or sent the wrong version to a client.\n\nAnd even if I can look at it and know it’s probably fine, my mind kind of circles back to it anyway. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control when these thoughts about mistakes come into your mind?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I can sometimes redirect myself, so it’s not like I’m completely helpless with it. But I don’t feel like I choose when it comes up.\n\nIt’s more like I’ll be trying to move on, and then the thought just comes back — that maybe I missed some tiny mistake in a layout, or sent the wrong version to a client. Even when I can tell myself it’s probably fine, my mind keeps circling back. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these nagging thoughts about mistakes in your work?\n\nPatient: I’m not exactly sure when I’d say it started… it’s been long enough that it’s what made me come in, I guess. The main thing is these nagging thoughts that I’ve missed some tiny mistake in a layout, or maybe sent the wrong version to a client.\n\nEven when I can look at it and think, “No, this is probably fine,” my mind keeps circling back to it. I don’t know if that answers the “how long” part very well, sorry.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors are interfering with your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t want to overstate it, because I am still getting my work done. It’s not like everything has fallen apart.\n\nBut I have started missing some internal deadlines, mostly because I keep checking or hesitating before I send things on. And I’ve noticed I’m playing it safer with designs than I used to. Like, I’ll hold back on a bolder idea because I’m worried I’ll miss something and it’ll reflect badly on me.\n\nI guess the part that bothers me is I’m trying to move toward a creative director role eventually, and I worry this is making me look less ready for that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and checking behaviors significantly affecting your ability to enjoy your work or feel confident in your professional abilities?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, at least somewhat. I still enjoy parts of the work, and I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function, because I am still getting things done.\n\nBut it has affected my confidence. I’ve started missing internal deadlines here and there because I’m checking things too much or hesitating before sending them. And I notice I’m choosing safer design ideas instead of the bolder ones I might have tried before, because I’m afraid I’ll miss something and it’ll make me look careless.\n\nI guess the bigger worry is that I want to keep moving toward a creative director role, and I’m scared this is making me seem less capable or less ready for that. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these thoughts and checking behaviors are causing you significant distress or are significantly interfering with your work or personal relationships?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s ruining things, exactly… I’m still getting my work done, and I don’t think it’s really affecting my personal relationships in a major way.\n\nBut at work, yes, it is getting in the way some. I’ve started missing internal deadlines because I keep checking or second-guessing before I send things off. And I’ve noticed I’m holding back on some of the bolder design ideas I used to feel more comfortable trying, because I’m worried I’ll overlook something and it’ll make me look careless.\n\nI guess the distress part is mostly that I’m worried it could hurt my chances of moving toward a creative director role. I don’t want to seem like I’m not ready for that. Does that count as significant?\n\nDoctor: Have these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors been present for a specific period or have they been ongoing for as long as you can remember?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure, honestly. I’m sorry, I know that’s probably not as specific as you’re looking for.\n\nDoctor: Have these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors been constant since you first noticed them, or have they come and go over time?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. And I notice I’m choosing safer design ideas instead of the bolder ones I might have tried before, because I’m afraid I’ll miss something and it’ll make me look careless.\n\nI guess the bigger worry is that I want to keep moving toward a creative director role, and I’m scared this is making me seem less capable or less ready for that. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these thoughts and checking behaviors are causing you significant distress or are significantly interfering with your work or personal relationships?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s ruining things, exactly… I’m still getting my work done, and I don’t think it’s really affecting my personal relationships in a major way.\n\nBut at work, yes, it is getting in the way some. I’ve started missing internal deadlines because I keep checking or second-guessing before I send things off. And I’ve noticed I’m holding back on some of the bolder design ideas I used to feel more comfortable trying, because I’m worried I’ll overlook something and it’ll make me look careless.\n\nI guess the distress part is mostly that I’m worried it could hurt my chances of moving toward a creative director role. I don’t want to seem like I’m not ready for that. Does that count as significant?\n\nDoctor: Have these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors been present for a specific period or have they been ongoing for as long as you can remember?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure, honestly. I don’t think it’s been there as long as I can remember… or at least not in this exact way.\n\nIt feels more like something I’ve noticed over time, but I’d have to think about when it really started. I’m sorry, I know that’s probably not as specific as you’re looking for.\n\nDoctor: Have these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors been constant since you first noticed them, or have they come and go over time?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I don’t think it feels constant every single day, but I also wouldn’t say it completely goes away either.\n\nIt’s more like… I notice it more at certain times, especially when I’m trying to finish something or send something out. But I haven’t really tracked it closely, so I don’t want to overstate it. Is that the kind of distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking your work repeatedly helps to reduce your anxiety about making mistakes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it does, at least for a little while. Like before I submit something, I’ll re-open the file, compare the layers, re-save it, and sometimes kind of mentally walk through every design choice again.\n\nIt’s not like it takes over my whole day or anything, but it does make me slower. And I guess it makes me feel a little safer in the moment, like I’m less likely to have missed something. But then it also makes me feel less free creatively, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to meet your responsibilities at work or in your personal life due to these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m still getting things done. I’ve missed some internal deadlines because I keep checking and adjusting before I’m willing to let something go. I mean, I can still function and get things done. But it does make me tense in the moment, like I can’t quite relax until I’ve checked it again. I’d call it more frustrating and uncomfortable than unbearable. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it is enough that I notice it and wish I could just submit things more easily.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and checking behaviors affecting your relationships with friends or family in any way?\n\nPatient: Not hugely with friends or family, I don’t think… at least not directly. I’m still functioning and getting my work done. It’s more that work is where it shows up — I’ve started missing some internal deadlines because I keep checking, and I’m holding back on bolder design ideas because I’m scared I’ll overlook something and hurt my chances of moving toward a creative director role. So I guess it affects my mood and confidence, which maybe they notice a bit, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe these thoughts about mistakes as popping into your head even when you're trying to think about something else?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. Not constantly, but they do sort of pop back in when I’m trying to move on to something else. Like I’ll be working on another layout, or even just trying to step away, and I get this nagging thought that maybe I missed some tiny mistake or sent the wrong version to a client.\n\nAnd even if I can look at it and know it’s probably fine, my mind kind of circles back to it anyway. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control when these thoughts about mistakes come into your mind?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I can sometimes redirect myself, so it’s not like I’m completely helpless with it. But I don’t feel like I choose when it comes up.\n\nIt’s more like I’ll be trying to move on, and then the thought just comes back — that maybe I missed some tiny mistake in a layout, or sent the wrong version to a client. Even when I can tell myself it’s probably fine, my mind keeps circling back. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these nagging thoughts about mistakes in your work?\n\nPatient: I’m not exactly sure when I’d say it started… it’s been long enough that it’s what made me come in, I guess. The main thing is these nagging thoughts that I’ve missed some tiny mistake in a layout, or maybe sent the wrong version to a client.\n\nEven when I can look at it and think, “No, this is probably fine,” my mind keeps circling back to it. I don’t know if that answers the “how long” part very well, sorry.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors are interfering with your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t want to overstate it, because I am still getting my work done. It’s not like everything has fallen apart.\n\nBut I have started missing some internal deadlines, mostly because I keep checking or hesitating before I send things on. And I’ve noticed I’m playing it safer with designs than I used to. Like, I’ll hold back on a bolder idea because I’m worried I’ll miss something and it’ll reflect badly on me.\n\nI guess the part that bothers me is I’m trying to move toward a creative director role eventually, and I worry this is making me look less ready for that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and checking behaviors significantly affecting your ability to enjoy your work or feel confident in your professional abilities?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, at least somewhat. I still enjoy parts of the work, and I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function, because I am still getting things done.\n\nBut it has affected my confidence. I’ve started missing internal deadlines here and there because I’m checking things too much or hesitating before sending them. And I notice I’m choosing safer design ideas instead of the bolder ones I might have tried before, because I’m afraid I’ll miss something and it’ll make me look careless.\n\nI guess the bigger worry is that I want to keep moving toward a creative director role, and I’m scared this is making me seem less capable or less ready for that. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these thoughts and checking behaviors are causing you significant distress or are significantly interfering with your work or personal relationships?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s ruining things, exactly… I’m still getting my work done, and I don’t think it’s really affecting my personal relationships in a major way.\n\nBut at work, yes, it is getting in the way some. I’ve started missing internal deadlines because I keep checking or second-guessing before I send things off. And I’ve noticed I’m holding back on some of the bolder design ideas I used to feel more comfortable trying, because I’m worried I’ll overlook something and it’ll make me look careless.\n\nI guess the distress part is mostly that I’m worried it could hurt my chances of moving toward a creative director role. I don’t want to seem like I’m not ready for that. Does that count as significant?\n\nDoctor: Have these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors been present for a specific period or have they been ongoing for as long as you can remember?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure, honestly. I don’t think it’s been there as long as I can remember… or at least not in this exact way.\n\nIt feels more like something I’ve noticed over time, but I’d have to think about when it really started. I’m sorry, I know that’s probably not as specific as you’re looking for.\n\nDoctor: Have these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors been constant since you first noticed them, or have they come and go over time?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I don’t think it feels constant every single day, but I also wouldn’t say it completely goes away either.\n\nIt’s more like… I notice it more at certain times, especially when I’m trying to finish something or send something out. But I haven’t really tracked it closely, so I don’t want to overstate it. Is that the kind of distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking your work repeatedly helps to reduce your anxiety about making mistakes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it does, at least for a little while. Like before I submit something, I’ll re-open the file, compare the layers, re-save it, and sometimes kind of mentally walk through every design choice again.\n\nIt’s not like it takes over my whole day or anything, but it does make me slower. And I guess it makes me feel a little safer in the moment, like I’m less likely to have missed something. But then it also makes me feel less free creatively, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to meet your responsibilities at work or in your personal life due to these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m still getting things done. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m failing at work or anything.\n\nBut yes, I guess it has started to affect things a bit. I’ve missed some internal deadlines because I keep checking and adjusting before I’m willing to let something go. And I think I’ve been playing it safer with design choices than I normally would, because I’m worried I’ll miss something obvious or make a mistake.\n\nThat part bothers me, because I do want to keep moving toward more of a creative director role, and I worry this could make me seem less decisive or less ready. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing these checks to try and get rid of the anxious thoughts about mistakes?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think that’s fair to say. I don’t love admitting it, but before I submit something, I’ll re-open the file, compare the layers again, re-save it, and kind of mentally walk through the design choices one more time.\n\nIt’s not all day or anything like that, but it does seem like I’m doing it to quiet that “what if I missed something?” feeling. It helps briefly, but it also slows me down and makes me feel less free creatively.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
